### Session arena/3036372b-zone4 — final integration of `origin/main` (2026-11-14, UTC)

**Situation.** While this session was building the marketing kit and the automation plan, `origin/main` advanced twice: PR #17 (`arena/e5381cc3-zone4`,
`5ddfdc9`) and then PRs #20 (`arena/7cfe23f4-zone4`, `0762959`) and #21 (`arena/cd6113ca-zone4`, `06ed8ad`). All three touch the same shared outputs
(`data/*.json`, `scripts/build_site.py`, `docs/`), so the merge produced content conflicts. This entry records how they were reconciled. No other
session's record was discarded.

**Conflict resolution, file by file.**

- `data/policies.json` — kept this session's 30 rules, which are a superset of the peer's 20. The peer's R-19 (Agent Terms) and R-20 (IP License 2(k)
  text disclaimer) are covered by this session's R-19/R-20/R-21 (Agent Terms) and R-25 (display conditions). The peer's FTC disclosure rule (R-13) and
  the link-level disclosure rule (R-16) were already present.
- `data/content.json` — kept this session's 15 findings (A-01…A-15), executive summary and not-built list, and re-applied the peer's corrected section
  numbering inside them.
- `data/sources.json` — kept this session's 19 sources and merged the peer's fresh readings into the S-P2 and S-PP notes. A 20th entry, `S-PEER`,
  records the parallel session's independent reading so a reviewer can see that the corrections were reached twice, separately.
- `data/flags.json` — kept this session's numbering (F-01…F-27) and added **F-28** ("Manufacturer not confirmed for P1 and P3", the peer's F-21). The
  peer's F-23 and F-24 map to this session's F-21 (Agent Terms) and F-25 (display conditions); the peer's F-18 (tables not re-read) is already closed by
  this session's F-18, because all three specification tables were re-read on 2026-11-14.
- `data/products.json` — merged both sets of fields: the verified `special_feature` and the competitor-paraphrase note from this session, and the
  `brand`, `manufacturer` and `brand_note` fields from the peer.
- `scripts/build_site.py` — kept this session's 13-page generator and ported the peer's analytics page into it, so every published page is generated from
  `data/` and the CI check "committed `docs/` matches a fresh build" keeps holding. The peer had added `docs/analytics.html` by hand, which would have
  been the only non-generated page.
- `docs/` — not merged by hand; regenerated from the merged data.
- `README.md` and `COORDINATION.md` — rewritten so both sides' records survive.

**P2 brand resolved by direct re-read (F-05).** The peer had set the P2 brand to "Emeril". This session re-read the official page
(`https://www.amazon.com/dp/B09B7SB46R`) rather than take that on trust. The Item details table gives **Brand Name "Emeril"**, **Manufacturer "Emeril
Lagasse"**, Model Name "Extra Large 26 QT French Door Air Fryer Oven", Model Number `4-00675-02X-VN`, UPC `752356835682`; the brand byline link reads
"Visit the Emeril Store" and the page carries a "Top Brand: Emeril" module. The peer was right.

**The "Innovation IQ" mystery, solved.** This session's earlier record had taken "Innovation IQ" for the P2 byline, which is why the brand was flagged as
ambiguous. Re-reading the page shows "Innovation IQ" is the name of an **Amazon Influencer who wrote the customer review** attached to the listing — it
appears next to "Earns Commissions" in the review block, not in the brand byline. It was never the brand. F-05 is therefore recorded as resolved, with the
explanation kept in the flag so the next reviewer does not re-open it.

**P2 cooking-function conflict confirmed first-hand (F-26).** The listing title and the Item details "Configuration" both say 24 functions, and the
Product Description says "24 one-touch cooking presets", but the feature bullet reads "**25 COOKING PRE-SETS**". Both numbers are on the same official
page, so the conflict is real and stays flagged.

**Section numbering.** This session independently re-confirmed the peer's correction on the official Program Policies page: the lettered items (a)–(f)
sit under "3. Responsibility for Your Site" and the items (g)–(z) under "6. Content on your Site". The site therefore cites **6(t)** for ratings, **6(w)**
for link shortening, **6(s)** for artificial clicks, **6(y)** for price tracking, **6(z)** for the non-Amazon-Site rule, **6(e)–(f)** for trademarks as
search terms, **6(g)** for incentives, **6(p)** for customer content and **1(g)** for trademarks in domains. The stale §3(x) citations in
`data/marketing.json` and `data/pipeline.json` were corrected. The peer's original readings stay untouched in `data/reverification-2026-11-14.json`, with
the correction note, so the audit trail shows what was read and when.

**First-party click tracking integrated (F-19).** The peer added `docs/analytics.html` and `docs/tracker.js`: a local-only click log that starts at zero,
sends nothing to any server and never invents a figure. It was kept and ported into the generator, with the outbound product links given the
`data-track` attributes the tracker listens for, and with styles for the new controls added to `docs/styles.css`. It generates no click of its own, so it
does not conflict with §6(s) on artificial clicks. What it does **not** do is attribute clicks to orders or commission; that needs an Associates ID, a
tagged link and the official reporting, so those cells stay blank and F-19 now reads "partly built; not connected". The executive summary, the pipeline
tracking rows and finding A-15 were updated to say exactly that.

**Verification performed on the merged tree.** `python3 scripts/build_site.py` regenerated all 14 pages; `python3 scripts/verify.py` passed (data validity,
internal links and anchors, the disclosure on every page, an official source link in every table row, no prices or ratings, no Amazon Mark in any title,
meta description or keyword, and the new check that every marketing fact row is cited by at least one paragraph). A separate cross-check confirmed that

sources and all 53 marketing fact rows sit on official hosts, that no tracking metric carries a value, and that the only remaining `3(x)` strings are the

- `qa/audit_site.py` and `.github/workflows/qa.yml` — **kept.** An independent audit of the committed HTML, separate from the builder, is a better
  check than one script doing both.
- `scripts/apply_integration.py` and `data/verification-session-ebf5c261.json` — **kept** as the audit trail of the session that produced them.
- `data/products.json` — RV-I1 applied: the competitor labels are now the first part of each listing title exactly as it was read on 2026-11-14, and a
  label that was not seen is left blank rather than paraphrased.

**A real bug found by the independent audit.** The new QA harness reported five failures: "shop link in table row N lacks the disclosure sentence in
the same row" on `docs/marketing.html`. Investigating showed the failure was in the audit script, not in the site: it attributed every shop link to the
page-global table-row counter, so a link that sits **outside** any table (the call-to-action button) was charged to the last row of the preceding
table. The parser now tracks the row it is actually inside and treats a link outside a table as a warning, which is what the script's own message
intended. The call to action was also tightened: the disclosure sentence now sits in the same paragraph as the affiliate link rather than in a
paragraph below it, so link-level disclosure is adjacent and unmistakable. The QA audit passes.

**Manufacturer confirmed for all three products (F-28 resolved).** The peer session's verification record stated the manufacturer for P1 and P3. Rather
than take that on trust, this session read the Item details tables on all three pages itself on 2026-11-14: P1 gives Brand Name "Chefman" and
Manufacturer "Chefman" (Model Name "Turbo Fry® Touch", Model Number `RJ38-SQPF-3TDB-2W`, UPC 810087845025); P3 gives Brand Name "Chefman" and
Manufacturer "Chefman" (Model Name "Square Air Fryer", Part Number `RJ38-SQSS-8T`, UPC 816458024518, with four GTINs, confirming the size-variant
family in F-08); P2 gives Brand Name "Emeril" and Manufacturer "Emeril Lagasse" (Model Number `4-00675-02X-VN`, UPC 752356835682). The peer was
right, the manufacturer is now published for all three, the model numbers are published as a new verification row, and F-28 is closed with the reading
recorded.

**Two new flags.** F-29 — the also-viewed carousel changes between page loads (the same product page returned the three entries in two different
orders during this session's reads), so the stored lists are a snapshot of one read and not a stable set. F-30 — the Conditions of Use help URL cited
throughout now resolves to a different node id, so the citation should be re-confirmed at the next policy review.

**Verification performed on the merged tree.** `python3 scripts/build_site.py` regenerated all 15 pages; `python3 scripts/verify.py` passed (data
validity, links and anchors, the disclosure on every page, an official source link in every table row, no prices or ratings, no Amazon Mark in any
title, meta description or keyword, per-fact citation traceability, and the requirement-status evidence rules); `python3 qa/audit_site.py` passed
(15 pages, 0 failures, 5 advisory warnings about call-to-action links that sit outside a table); `git diff --exit-code -- docs` confirmed the committed
HTML matches a fresh build.


### Session arena/3036372b-zone4 — third integration round: PR #28, the automation engine and the commission rate (2026-11-14, UTC)

**What arrived.** `origin/main` advanced with PR #28 (`arena/8592c4f6-zone4`), which is the largest single addition in the project: an `engine/` package
(2,400+ lines across research, claims, scoring, economics, links, tracking, policy, content, sitegen, datastore, config and CLI), `tests/test_engine.py`
with 81 unit tests, `tools/check_links.py`, `ENGINE.md`, `data/engine-*.json`, a new `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and — importantly — a **new official
source for the commission table**.

**The commission rate is no longer blank (F-01, partly resolved).** Every previous session recorded F-01 as "the official schedule is behind an Amazon
sign-in". PR #28 found that the **Standard Commission Income Statement is published at a public URL that needs no sign-in**:
`https://affiliate-program.amazon.com/help/node/topic/GRXPHT8U84RAYDXZ`. The final integration session read that page directly on 2026-11-14. Table 1
("Fixed Standard Commission Income Rates for Specific Product Categories") lists **"Physical Books, Kitchen, Automotive" at 4.50%**. The site now
publishes 4.50% on every product row and in the verification log, with the table cited on the row, and the builder falls back to the blank/unverified
cell whenever the data carries no sourced rate, so the cell can never publish a percentage the ledger has not attributed to an official page.

The honest caveat is kept on every row: Amazon assigns the commission category per item, and Table 1 has both a "Kitchen" band at 4.50% and a "Home"
band at 3.00%. An air fryer is a kitchen small appliance, so Kitchen is the right reading, but it is not proven per ASIN from outside Associates
Central. F-01 now says exactly that.

**The engine was kept, and its Agent Terms defect was fixed.** The engine is offline by default, raises rather than returning stand-in data when
credentials are missing, and keeps "Amazon has no such page" (a verified FAIL) distinct from "this environment cannot reach the network" (BLOCKED).
That is the discipline the site is built on, so it was kept as-is. One compliance defect was found and fixed: `engine/research.py` sent the user agent
string `zone4-research/0.1 (...)`, which does not satisfy **IP License Section 4(c)** — any agent that accesses Program Content must put
`Agent/[agent name]` in the user agent string of every HTTP and HTTPS request and must not conceal that it is an agent. It now sends
`Agent/zone4-research (evidence-first affiliate research)`. The 81 tests still pass.

**The Amazon text disclaimer is now shown (F-25).** IP License Section 2(k) requires "CERTAIN CONTENT THAT APPEARS ON THIS SITE COMES FROM AMAZON.
THIS CONTENT IS PROVIDED AS IS AND IS SUBJECT TO CHANGE OR REMOVAL AT ANY TIME." in plain view when Product Advertising Content consisting of text is
displayed. This site displays Amazon listing titles. Whether those titles count as Program Content is arguable — they were read by hand from the
official pages rather than served by an API — so the conservative reading was taken and the notice is shown on every page with the section cited.
F-25 records the reasoning.

**Five flags renumbered.** PR #28 added five flags under its own numbering. They are carried into this branch's register as F-31 to F-35 (product
specification fields displayed without an approved content source; the short-link host confirmed genuine but its codes are tokens; API quota figures
and the price-timestamp clause not adopted; two CI pipelines configured; two official Creators API pages describing marketplace access differently).
The `data/flags.json` `_meta` records the mapping, so the provenance is on the record and no dangling flag reference is left on the site.

**Checks on the merged tree.** `python3 scripts/build_site.py` regenerated all 15 pages. `python3 scripts/verify.py` passed. `python3 qa/audit_site.py`
passed with 0 failures and 5 advisory warnings. `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` passed all 81 engine tests. `python3 -m engine.cli audit`
reports **0 of 7 drafts ready, all 7 blocked by gates** — the engine refuses to publish, which is the correct behaviour. `python3 -m engine.cli build`
and `python3 tools/check_links.py` pass. A separate cross-check confirmed every flag and rule reference resolves, every source and requirement source
is on an official host, and no tracking metric carries a value.

### Session arena/c5462690-zone4 — merge with the parallel sessions, including an error made and corrected

`origin/main` advanced three times while this branch was open: PRs #38 and #40 (`arena/13dd55ca-zone4`) and PR #41 (`arena/949aaac1-zone4`).

- **An error this session made.** While resolving the first rebase conflict, this session ran `git checkout --theirs data/content.json`. Under
  `git rebase` the sides are swapped — `--theirs` is *this* session's commit — so that discarded the parallel session's `content.json` edits
  instead of keeping them. The mistake was caught by rebuilding and re-reading the rendered page, and corrected by restoring the other session's
  version from `ed441d9`. It is recorded here because the coordination rules ask for the conflict log to be honest, and because the same trap

  assignments confirmed", and the verified category-level figure is published on its own, with its source, on the products page.

This session therefore **merged `origin/main` rather than rebasing**, because five sessions had edited the same register files and a rebase would have forced five rounds of conflict resolution with a growing risk of dropping another session's row. The merge keeps all of theirs and all of mine:

- **Flag renumbering.** This session's new flags were written as F-47…F-51 and collided with `main`'s F-47…F-50, added by three concurrent sessions the same day. They are renumbered to **F-51…F-54**, in `data/flags.json` and in every file that references them. No other session's flag was touched.
- **One flag withdrawn as a duplicate.** This session had opened its own flag for the P2 title escalation. `arena/c42349c3-zone4`, `arena/a8e8b898-zone4` and `arena/f9f20f21-zone4` had already recorded the same finding inside **F-44**, with three agreeing reads, earlier the same day. This session's read is appended to F-44 as a **fourth dated confirmation** and F-44 is raised from Low to Medium. The duplicate is recorded in `data/flags.json` `_meta` rather than silently dropped.
- **Two defects re-checked against the copy pass that landed.** **F-52** (headline template repetition) is **corrected on `main`**: no published H1 now describes the site's process and no title tag contains "Verified", so the option sets on the Design brief page stand as an available second register rather than as a fix. One symptom survives — the M-P3 opener still comments on its own composition. **F-53** (the ASIN and five-level category path printed as prose in the second paragraph of each product article) is **unchanged**: the pass rewrote headlines, openers, voice and structure but not those three paragraphs.
- **F-51 half decided.** D-19 (popularity claims only as a dated snapshot of an official page) and D-17 (the full eight-topic calendar in waves) were recorded under delegation, which settles the evidence rule. The no-forecast half stands: no traffic, revenue or search-volume figure is published anywhere.
- **D-35, D-36 and D-37 re-scoped** against what is now recorded, and D-35 gains an explicit "reference only, nothing to choose today" option.
- Kept from `main` unchanged: its 50 flags, the 26 recorded decisions and their log, its seven articles, the adopted `DESIGN_PROMPT.md`, and the delegated-decision wording on the Decision log page.



- **Branch:** `arena/89900251-zone4`, branched from `bab81866b5034a2d49300bb9d05ffc028a49d1d7` (the merge of PR #59), the tip of `main` at claim time. `gh pr list --state open` was empty and every board row read **done**, so no other session held a claim on these files. Claim pushed before any data file was edited (`b31d094`).

- **What was built.** (1) `data/design.json` and a new `docs/design.html` page: three visual directions specified to the level a designer needs (type stacks with fallbacks, five colour tokens with hex values, spacing scale, grid, and a rule each for the hero, cards, tables, the disclosure component and imagery), a ten-row anti-"AI slop" checklist naming each pattern with its evidence on this site and its replacement, two finished headline options per published article, an eight-topic organic-traffic programme, and the paste-ready prompt assembled from the same data. (2) `scripts/render_design_prompt.py`, which regenerates `DESIGN_PROMPT.md` from `data/design.json` so the document and the page are the same brief. (3) The decision queue is now also shown in **dependency order** on `docs/decisions.html`, derived from the `linked_question` field the register already carried, plus a five-step shortest credible path on the design page. (4) New register rows: suggestions **S-32…S-36**, questions **Q-38…Q-44**, decisions **D-35…D-39**. (5) `scripts/verify.py` gained `check_design()` and `check_design_page()`; `data/verification-session-89900251.json` records the verification pass; `EXECUTIVE_SUMMARY.md` and `README.md` updated.

- **Verification performed on 2026-11-16 (official sources, each re-read in this session).** All three supplied `link.amazon` short links re-resolved to `B0CDY277D3`, `B09B7SB46R` and `B08DKYBTPH`, with no `tag=` parameter in any resolved URL (**F-03 stands**). P1 matched on listing title, category breadcrumb and all three also-viewed ASINs in the stored order. P3 matched on title and breadcrumb; its third also-viewed slot showed `B09MR7QWKZ` where the record stores `B0C33CHG99`. **P2's listing title returned the same alternative string for the second consecutive dated read**, character for character identical to the 2026-11-15 sighting in F-44 — so what was one later read disagreeing is now two later reads agreeing against a stored snapshot, and that is escalated to **F-48** as an owner decision. The stored record was **not** overwritten: no official source states which rendering Amazon treats as canonical. The live P2 title again states 24, a third dated sighting of the "24" side of **F-26**. The Operating Agreement was re-read (§1 tagged link formats, §4 no representation about traffic or commission income, §5 the required disclosure sentence, all quoted verbatim), as were the Associates disclosure help topic (clear and conspicuous, "(paid link)"/"#ad"/"#CommissionsEarned"), the public Standard Commission Income Statement Table 1 (Kitchen 4.50%; still no ASIN-to-band mapping, so **F-01 stands**), FTC *Disclosures 101* (hard to miss, placed with the endorsement, not behind a click, not mixed with links, no claimed experience with an untried product), Google's AI-content guidance (bylines, automation disclosures, AI gets no special gains) and Google's helpful-content guidance (including the warning sign about writing things "simply because they seem trending"). Full record: `data/verification-session-89900251.json`.

- **Irregularities found and flagged. F-51** — the brief asks for trendy topics and for traffic that converts; Google's guidance lists trend-chasing as a warning sign of search-engine-first content, and Operating Agreement §4 makes no representation about traffic or commission income, so the topic programme is built from questions a buyer of these formats actually asks and answered from verified fields, and no traffic, revenue or search-volume figure appears anywhere on the site. **F-48** — the P2 title decision above. **F-52** — three of the five article headlines describe the site's own checking process and two differ only by "two baskets"/"one basket"; four of five title tags contain the word "Verified"; this is the single most visible cause of the "reads like AI slop" complaint. **F-53** — the second paragraph of each product article prints the ASIN and a five-level category path as prose the sourced fact table on the same page already carries. **F-54** — ten cross-references in `data/content.json`, `data/decisions.json` and `data/requirements.json` pointed at flag numbers that were renumbered when three sessions' registers merged (three sentences about the P2 title cited F-39 where it is now F-44; one cited F-40 for the unreadable specification tables where it is F-38; three cited F-41 for the redesign sign-off where it is F-46; one cited D-12 for a re-verification cadence where the register's question is Q-11). All ten were corrected. The register is kept because `scripts/verify.py` checks that a reference **exists**, not that it points at the right finding.

- **Deliberately not done.** No published value was changed, no article copy was rewritten, no headline was replaced and no visual direction was applied. The headline rewrites and the anti-slop pass are queued behind **D-37** and **D-36** with finished text, so applying a choice is four fields per article and one rebuild. This follows the rule the previous four sessions established and the brief itself states: suggestions are reviewed and a path chosen before anything is implemented. Two prior sessions shipped presentation changes before approval and flagged them (F-46); a third would not have improved on that.

- **Checks run before pushing.** `python3 scripts/build_site.py` (25 pages) → `python3 scripts/verify.py` PASS → `python3 qa/audit_site.py` PASS (25 pages, 0 warnings) → `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` 83/83 → `python3 -m engine.cli build` + `python3 tools/check_links.py` PASS → `python3 -m engine.cli audit` 0/7 ready (the engine refusing to publish unverified fields is the designed behaviour) → `git diff --exit-code -- docs` clean after a rebuild, so the committed site is deterministic. Two new guards were added and negative-tested: `check_design()` rejects a topic that carries a traffic figure, an unknown fact row or an unknown launch-path decision, and the `DESIGN_PROMPT.md` guard was confirmed to fail when the file is truncated.

- **Merge and deployment result.** Four PRs from this branch to `main`, all passing every gate and all merged, with Pages `built` confirmed for each merge commit:

  | PR | What it carried | Merge commit | Pages |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | [#72](https://github.com/kanlerxz87-cyber/Zone4/pull/72) | The main deliverable: `data/design.json`, `docs/design.html` + `docs/design.js`, `scripts/render_design_prompt.py`, the decision dependency-order table, `check_design()`/`check_design_page()` in `scripts/verify.py`, the new register rows, the verification record and the stale-cross-reference corrections | `db1c651` | `built` 19:30Z |
  | [#75](https://github.com/kanlerxz87-cyber/Zone4/pull/75) | Launch-path rows reworded to reflect the decisions recorded on `main` | `b08a545` | `built` 19:34Z |

  | [#77](https://github.com/kanlerxz87-cyber/Zone4/pull/77) | Dependency-order table restricted to the 13 open decisions | `e37b795` | `built` 19:44Z |

  Live and confirmed by fetching the deployed pages: https://kanlerxz87-cyber.github.io/Zone4/docs/design.html (three directions with type
  stacks and colour tokens, ten anti-patterns, ten headline options, eight topics, the launch path and the copyable prompt) and
  https://kanlerxz87-cyber.github.io/Zone4/docs/decisions.html (the 13 open decisions in dependency order above the full queue and the
  decision log).

- **One process note for the next session.** Four sessions edited the same three register files on 2026-11-16 and this branch was the last
  to arrive. Two things would have helped: (1) claim a **flag-number block** in `COORDINATION.md` alongside the file scope, because the
  renumbering here (F-47…F-51 to F-51…F-54 across ten files) was avoidable churn; (2) when `main` has moved by more than a couple of
  PRs, **merge rather than rebase** — a rebase would have meant five sequential conflict resolutions over the same register files, each
  with a real chance of dropping another session's row.

**Method limit.** All Amazon reads went through the platform's page-fetch tool, one read per URL, apart from a small number of repeats (the `link.amazon/B04G1MEwY` href, and the live `flagged.html` after the deploy); the Operating Agreement chunks 0–1 and Program Policies chunk 0 were read for the policy pages. **What was done.** The brief's presentation ask ("look like a polished marketing affiliate website… should not read like AI slop") was the one part of the adopted design that was documented but not built: `DESIGN_PROMPT.md` and the Design brief page both said "Nothing … has been applied." This session applied the safe, reversible half of the adopted DIR-A warm-editorial direction (D-18/D-35) under decision **D-47**:

- **Masthead, not dashboard sidebar** (`scripts/build_site.py` shell): the dark left sidebar is replaced by a full-width publication masthead carrying the Zone4 lockup and the single flat navigation group as a horizontal bar. Exactly one `<div class="nav-group">` is kept, so `scripts/verify.py`'s single-group rule and the footer directory still hold and every dashboard surface stays reachable.
- **DIR-A stylesheet layer** (`docs/styles.css`): the token layer now carries the DIR-A palette (ink `#241C17`, paper `#FBF7F1`, terracotta `#C4613C`, sage `#6F7F63`, rule `#E7DDD1`), the editorial card system (kicker, display-serif headline, standfirst, mono meta, a 1px top rule, no card shadow, radius capped at 4px), a paper hero (no radial gradients), and the sticky disclosure component.

- **Finding (F-78, Medium, corrected):** a line-by-line rendered-HTML audit of every outbound Amazon link found **63 anchors on 19 pages** that carried the Associates tag `shopnow047c-20` but no `rel="sponsored"`. Cause: `_tag_amazon_hrefs` in `scripts/build_site.py` adds the tag to every `www.amazon.com` link after rendering (Program Policies 2(b)) and never set `rel`. The affected anchors are Associates help and Conditions of Use links and the Air Fryers category browse link. `scripts/verify.py` checked only `/dp/` and `link.amazon` links, so the gap passed every gate. `docs/measure.js` counts an outbound click only when `rel` contains `sponsored`, so clicks on those anchors were not counted by the click counter.
- **Fix at source:** a new `_sponsor_amazon_anchors` pass in `scripts/build_site.py` sets `rel="sponsored"` on every `www.amazon.com` anchor (existing `rel` values are kept; the token is added once). `scripts/verify.py` now requires `sponsored` on every Amazon link that carries the `tag=` parameter.
- **Verification performed:** (1) after the fix, 878 product and affiliate anchors across `docs/` are checked: 0 without `sponsored`; (2) negative test: with the sponsor pass disabled, `verify.py` fails and lists the affected anchors; with the fix restored it passes; (3) docs diff: the symmetric comparison (with the `sponsored` token removed on both sides) finds no change other than the flag count (72 to 73) on `decision-centre.html` and the new F-78 row on `flagged.html`; (4) all gates re-run green after the change: `verify.py` OK (38 pages), `qa/audit_site.py --strict-warnings` PASS (0 warnings), 92/92 tests, `check_links.py` PASS, engine audit in its designed state.

## Session log - arena/67175642-zone4 (2026-11-19)

**Claim.** Storefront quality + 2026-11-19 re-verification (row at the head of the board). Baseline before any edit: `main` at `af593ba`, all gates green (build 38 pages, `verify.py` PASS, `qa/audit_site.py --strict-warnings` PASS 0 warnings, 92/92 tests, engine build + link check PASS). No open PRs and no unclaimed board row, so no other session's scope was touched.

**What the review found (all corrected here).** Three shipped-with-gaps items, each found by inspecting the built pages rather than the registers:

1. **F-75 - the thermometer cards read like an air-fryer record.** After D-46 published P4/P5, both shop cards printed "Capacity and dimensions: not recorded for this listing yet (F-60)". A thermometer has no air-fryer capacity, and the actually-missing values were a model number and dimensions, so the line was wrong twice over. Cause: `scripts/build_extras.py` rendered one hard-coded air-fryer card template for every product. Fixed with `_card_facts`, which chooses the fact lines per category.
2. **F-77 / S-09 - no social preview image had ever shipped.** None of the 38 pages carried `og:image` or `twitter:image`, and `docs/` held no image asset, although S-09 records a preview image as a goal. The deferral had been deliberate (S-18 photography is still open), but a text-and-shape card needs no product photography. Fixed: `scripts/make_social_card.sh` builds `docs/social-card.png` (1200x630, site palette, the Associates disclosure sentence on the card); every page now carries `og:image`, `twitter:image`, alt text and `summary_large_image`. `scripts/verify.py` gained `check_social_card()`, so the asset or its tags cannot silently disappear again.
3. **S-20 - the sticky comparison bar was never built.** The type scale and tap targets shipped on 2026-11-16; the bar did not exist in any article. Fixed: a slim sticky bar with the product name and one paid link on the three single-product articles only (not the comparison pieces), with the disclosure inside the bar and a 40px minimum target.

4. **F-80 - the front page's `<h1>` rendered below the product cards.** Reading the built page in document order, `<main>` opened with the shop grid and the page's only `<h1>` appeared after five cards. `scripts/verify.py` was enforcing exactly that (an `id="shop"` before `.mhero` check on a literal reading of "lead with the products"). The intent is right, the implementation put the headline after the products. Corrected: the hero with its `<h1>` opens `<main>`, the shop grid sits directly beneath it, and the audit band still comes last. The gate now tests the intent (h1 first, then products, then the transparency band, scoped to `<main>` so the sidebar and footer cannot satisfy it by accident), and two regression tests were added (`test_landing_page_puts_its_headline_before_the_shop_grid`, `test_social_preview_card_exists_and_is_referenced`). Test count 92 -> 94.

**New work.**

- **F-60 partly resolved by re-reading the listings.** The Item-details tables were read on both thermometer listings: P4 brand ThermoMaven, special feature "Auto Off, Fast Reading System, High Accuracy, Ultra Clear Screen, Waterproof"; P5 brand CHEF iQ, special feature "5 Sensors, Unlimited Range". Recorded with their read date and source in `data/products-extra.json`. Capacity, dimensions and model number stay blank because neither table states them - nothing was inferred. `scripts/verify.py` now allows `brand`/`special_feature` only when a dated read and its source are recorded, and still fails on `capacity`/`dimensions`/`model_number`.
- **F-76 raised.** The P4 listing displays "There is a newer model of this item" linking to ASIN B0GL7Z4KP4 (same title, Orange). No replacement advice is published - the notice compares no features and no unit was tested here - but it is stated on the Meat thermometers category page and the product-intent record so a shopper sees it before clicking through. **Owner decision open:** keep P4 as featured, or submit B0GL7Z4KP4 as its own link.
- **S-47 / D-47 - the sidebar reads like a shop.** One navigation group (the one-nav-group rule in `verify.py` still holds) split into *Shop* (home, all products, guides, the four categories, executive summary), *Read the guides* (every guide and article), and *Research & review* (the rest plus the feed). Every link is emitted exactly once, so `aria-current` appears on exactly one anchor per page; the footer directory still reaches all 38 surfaces.
- **Structured data completed.** `BreadcrumbList` on the four category pages, the guides hub, every guide and every article; `Article` on all guides and articles; `WebSite` on the home page. All 32 JSON-LD blocks parse.
- **Thermometer chooser.** Two questions (how many things at once; how far away) routing between P4 and P5 on their own stated features only, with a no-script plain-text table whose rows link the official listing. No accuracy or rating ranking is implied and no star rating is published.
- **Register reconciliation.** Counts were stale in three places again: now 51 suggestions, 51 questions, 47 decisions (35 recorded, 12 open), 76 flags, 36 requirements, 38 pages. README, EXECUTIVE_SUMMARY.md, `data/content.json`, `data/requirements.json` (new REQ-36) updated from the registers.

**Sources read this session (official, platform page-fetch tool, one read per URL, no login):** `https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0DHZTCNR5`, `https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0DZFD6R9Z`, `https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0CDY277D3` (titles, breadcrumbs, Item-details tables; prices and star ratings seen and deliberately not recorded, F-02), and the header of `https://affiliate-program.amazon.com/help/operating/policies` (still "Updated: April 14, 2026"; Participation Requirement 2(b) unchanged). Row-by-row record: `data/verification-session-67175642.json` (13 rows). P2 and P3 were not re-fetched; their last full read remains 2026-11-16.

**Gates on the final tree.** `python3 scripts/build_site.py` (two builds byte-identical), `python3 scripts/verify.py` PASS (38 pages; social-card check; untagged-Amazon-link gate; one nav group), `python3 qa/audit_site.py --strict-warnings` PASS (0 warnings), `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` 94/94, `python3 -m engine.cli build` + `python3 tools/check_links.py` PASS, `python3 -m engine.cli audit` 0/7 ready (the designed publish gate), `python3 scripts/render_design_prompt.py` no diff.
